# A-025 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-025-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-024 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> Every map leaves something out so that something else becomes easier to find.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

“Every map” sets the scope. “Leaves something out” establishes selectivity rather than exhaustive representation. “So that” gives that omission an enabling purpose or function. “Something else becomes easier to find” specifies improved findability, not simply absence.

1. Maps are incomplete. Incomplete: loses the omission's enabling function.
2. Everything omitted is worthless. Invents a value judgment.
3. Every map's selective omission serves to make other content easier to find. Preserves scope, means and improvement.
4. A map guarantees every search succeeds. Invents universal success and enlarges “something else” to everything.

Candidate 3 survives. The generic functional formulation preserves “so that” without requiring a named user's actual successful search or an explicit cartographer's mental intention.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove the finding contribution: incompleteness remains without its enabling point. Remove omission: easier finding remains without the specified means. |
| Alteration | “Harder to find” reverses the effect. “Everything else” overstates the beneficiary range. |
| Narrowing | One particular map would lose the universal scope. One named omitted item would add a specificity not supplied. |
| Reversal | Easier finding causing omission changes the explanatory direction; omitted and findable content are not interchangeable. |
| Dependency | Selective omission is the means through which the map improves findability. No particular graphic mechanism or sole sufficient cause is specified. |
| Sufficiency-location | The map's selective presentation is the particular enabling locus; no network of maps or distributed search sequence is established. |
| Removal test | Something is absent, but the source does not describe a formerly authoritative rule or constraint ceasing to govern. Omission alone is insufficient to infer that history. |
| Minimal invalidation | Omission remains but serves no easier finding of other content: the completed enabling function disappears. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | What the map's omission accomplishes for finding other content. |
| What changed? | Selective presentation is established as facilitating finding. |
| What made it sufficient? | Omission and improved findability are joined as means and enabling function. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | At each map's selective presentation as the particular means of facilitation. |
| What remains achieved? | Improved findability through that selectivity. |
| What would invalidate it? | The omission remains but does not serve to make anything else easier to find. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** an enabling function of selective presentation, not mere incompleteness or a completed search.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** omission is the presented means. A detached omission or an independently easier search would omit this specified function.
3. **Operative continuation:** yes; easier finding is expressly the function made available. Actual successful exercise is unnecessary, and no particular searcher is asserted.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** preserve selective absence while remove its facilitation of other finding.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** every map's omission serves improved findability of other content.

**What obtains through what is:** easier finding is enabled through selective presentation.

The relation is necessary, but the achieved identity is its practical facilitation of finding. Neither a merely established absence nor a conditional description without that facilitation captures the completion. This function is explicit rather than inferred from general usefulness of maps.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **Every map's selective omission serves to make other content easier to find.**

**Opening constraint:** the function of leaving content out is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** omission is understood as a means of improved findability.
**Constitutive organisation:** selective presentation facilitates locating other content.
**Location of sufficiency:** each map's selective presentation as the privileged enabling locus.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is improved findability through each map's selective presentation; it holds as this achievement because leaving some content out serves to make other content easier to locate; removing that facilitation would leave omission without the enabling function established.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** selective absence remains but does not serve easier finding.
**Confirmation:** preserves every map, some omission, other content and easier finding; adds no exhaustive beneficiary range, actual search success, omitted-content worthlessness or formerly governing rule; identifies one enabling function.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**12 — Branching output; Opening × Focal × Efficacy.**

Selective presentation constitutes a particular means through which finding becomes more readily executable. The achieved opening is that facilitation itself, rather than absence of content considered separately.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Opening fits improved practical accessibility of content. Relating is the strongest rival, but a means–effect connection alone omits the governing improvement in findability. Differentiation would make exclusion itself govern; Repair adds a failed correctness criterion; Integration adds roles in a whole; Settlement adds closure of a demand. |
| Topology | Focal: each selective presentation is the enabling locus. Distributed would require access across a constitutive field or course, not the unspecified other content given here. Release would require former governing force losing authority; selective absence alone does not establish that. |
| Persistence | Efficacy: easier finding is expressly the enabling function. Standing would leave available content without the improvement's practical identity. Bearing would preserve dependence through presentation but not the accomplished facilitation that defines the frozen achievement. |

### Strongest rivals

**21 — Responsive return; Relating × Focal × Efficacy.** Fits influence from one source, but the record identifies the particular achieved change as greater accessibility for finding. Replacing it with an unspecified consequential influence loses the form of opening confirmed.

**18 — Edge opening; Opening × Release × Efficacy.** Omission may resemble removal of obstruction. The frozen record supplies no former rule, authority or limit losing governance. It supplies selectivity as the enabling locus. Removal is not automatically Release.

**10 — Source field; Opening × Focal × Standing.** Would retain an available avenue of access. The record makes improved executability of finding, not merely the existence of represented content, the achieved identity.

“Branching output” does not require literal branches or multiple paths. The canonical conjunction establishes focal generative opening; the name cannot add a branching map design.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 12. **Confidence:** medium-high in explicit enabling function; moderate in transformation boundary. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen account; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-025-D1:** Test Opening against Relating without flattening improved findability into a generic effect or inventing new routes.
- **A-025-D2:** Preserve generic enabling function without claiming an actual successful search. Test Focal against Release without treating omission itself as de-governance.

# A-026 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-026-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-025 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> The bee visits a flower, and an orchard's future changes almost invisibly.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

A bee visits a flower. The continuation extends that small occurrence into a change in an orchard's future. “Almost invisibly” qualifies how unobtrusively the change occurs.

1. A bee visits a flower. Loses the consequence and scale.
2. Two unrelated events happen near one another. Grammatically possible but leaves their completed point unexplained.
3. The bee's flower visit has an almost imperceptible consequence for the orchard's future. Preserves the local occurrence's consequential reach.
4. The bee guarantees a successful orchard harvest through a specified pollination mechanism. Adds outcome, guarantee and detailed mechanism.

Candidate 3 is selected as the ordinary unified reading. The conjunction alone is not a general rule proving causation: this reading rests on the utterance presenting the orchard-future change as the significance of the small visit. The record does not strengthen that into a complete biological explanation. If only unrelated conjunction were intended, that would be a different Centre.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove orchard future: the visit remains without consequential reach. Remove the visit: a future change remains without its particular initiating occurrence. |
| Alteration | Replace “changes” with “remains unaffected”: the visit's presented significance disappears. Replace almost invisible with conspicuous: effect persists but unobtrusiveness changes. |
| Narrowing | Change orchard future to the flower's present position: loses the extension in scale and time. |
| Reversal | Let the orchard's already changed future explain the visit: reverses the presented direction. |
| Dependency | The future-change is understood as consequentially connected to this visit. No closed loop, exclusive cause or necessary sufficient mechanism is supplied. |
| Sufficiency-location | The local visit is the initiating point whose reach into the orchard's future completes the point. A whole ecosystem's coordinated operation is not asserted. |
| Minimal invalidation | The visit makes no difference to the orchard's future: the local occurrence survives without the governing consequence. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | What significance the small flower visit has beyond the local occurrence. |
| What changed? | It is understood through an almost imperceptible consequence for the orchard's future. |
| What made it sufficient? | Local occurrence and larger future-change form one consequential account. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | At the bee's visit as the particular initiating occurrence, specified through its consequential reach. |
| What remains achieved? | The visit's unobtrusive efficacy for the orchard's future. |
| What would invalidate it? | The visit leaves that future unaffected. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** an almost imperceptible consequential difference extending from a local occurrence to a larger future.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** the visit and future-change identify the consequence. The record does not claim the orchard's entire future depends exclusively on the bee.
3. **Operative continuation:** yes; a change to the future is the express completed point, not a reader's optional application.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** preserve the visit while deny any difference to that future.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** the visit is consequential for the orchard's future in an almost imperceptible way.

**What obtains through what is:** a difference to that future occurs through the visit's influence.

The result is not merely an established link or a future described through a static counterpart. Its identity is the difference the small occurrence makes. No particular benefit or measurable harvest is required.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **A bee's visit to a flower makes an almost imperceptible difference to an orchard's future.**

**Opening constraint:** the local visit's larger significance is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** its unobtrusive consequential reach into the orchard's future is established.
**Constitutive organisation:** the small initiating occurrence is understood through the larger future it affects.
**Location of sufficiency:** the visit as the particular source of the consequential difference.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the visit's almost imperceptible difference to the orchard's future; it holds as this achievement because the local occurrence is consequential for that future; removing the difference it makes would leave the visit without the larger significance established.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** the visit occurs but leaves the orchard's future unaffected.
**Confirmation:** preserves bee, flower, orchard, future change and near-invisibility; adds neither guaranteed benefit, exclusive cause, pollination mechanism nor feedback loop; identifies one consequential account.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**21 — Responsive return; Relating × Focal × Efficacy.**

A particular initiating occurrence is established as consequential for a larger continuation. Its source-centred influence is the relation, the locus of completion and the achieved consequential difference together.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Relating fits directed consequential influence. Opening would add enlarged availability; Differentiation would foreground a distinction alone; Repair adds a failed criterion; Integration adds coordinated parts; Settlement adds discharged demand. |
| Topology | Focal fits the particular source occurrence. Distributed would require a constitutive pattern of coupling across a field or course, not merely a large affected object. Release has no displaced governance. |
| Persistence | Efficacy fits the future difference expressly identified as achievement. Standing would retain a connection without the consequential difference. Bearing would retain a constitutive dependence without the changed continuation that governs this record. |

### Strongest rivals

**24 — Relational cycle; Relating × Distributed × Efficacy.** The orchard is broad in scale, but the record gives one initiating occurrence and its reach. It supplies no mutually coupled system, propagation pattern across stages or returning cycle. The size of the affected whole does not itself make completion Distributed.

**19 — Direct bearing; Relating × Focal × Standing.** Would retain the established connection. Here the decisive counterfactual identifies the difference made to continuation as indispensable to the achieved identity.

**20 — Pivoted balance; Relating × Focal × Bearing.** Would make counterpart-constituted identity govern. The frozen record instead identifies a consequential change from the initiating occurrence.

“Responsive return” does not require a reply or feedback. The canonical conjunction permits the focal source's influence upon resulting changes. No harvest guarantee is supplied.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 21. **Confidence:** moderate. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-026-D1:** Independently assess the Gate One choice of unified consequential reading versus bare conjunction. Any changed reading requires a new Centre investigation and separately preserved revision, not a source appeal during cell selection.
- **A-026-D2:** Test Focal against Distributed without treating the orchard's size as decisive or inventing a pollination chain or feedback cycle.

No earlier record or revision. Next target: A-027.

# A-027 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-027-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-026 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> There are places where winter silence makes a distant footstep sound enormous.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

“There are places where” asserts existence of settings, not a rule about all places. “Winter silence” specifies the setting's acoustic character. A “distant footstep” supplies a small, remote occurrence. “Sound enormous” gives it an extraordinary perceived scale through that quiet setting.

1. A footstep is audible. Too weak: omits its enormous impression and constitutive setting.
2. Winter silence physically amplifies the footstep's emitted energy. Adds an acoustic mechanism and replaces appearance with a physical measurement.
3. In some places a distant footstep has an enormous perceived scale through winter silence. Preserves existence, season, distance, perception and contextual determination.
4. All winter places make every footstep deafening. Adds universality, frequency and literal intensity.

Candidate 3 survives. “Enormous” is not a decibel value or a claim that the footstep becomes physically large.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove winter silence: an enormous-sounding step remains without the frame that gives the impression its identity. Remove enormousness: mere audibility replaces the distinctive perceptual determination. |
| Alteration | Replace silence with loud traffic: the asserted frame changes, without by itself proving what a step must sound like there. Replace “sounds enormous” with “is physically enlarged”: perception becomes a different physical claim. |
| Narrowing | Restrict the account to a nearby step: loses distance. Expand it to every place: overstates the existential scope. |
| Reversal | A footstep making winter silence sound enormous changes the term receiving the perceptual scale. |
| Dependency | The enormous impression is constituted against the quiet frame, rather than supplied as an absolute magnitude independently measured. |
| Sufficiency-location | The distant footstep's perceived scale is the particular determination completed through its acoustic setting. A distributed route or set of observers is not required. |
| Continuation test | No sound-travel improvement, new hearing capacity or subsequent behaviour is asserted. The result is the contextual appearance itself. |
| Minimal invalidation | The setting remains quiet but the distant footstep no longer has that enormous impression: this perceptual determination fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | How a distant footstep is perceived in the specified winter quiet. |
| What changed? | It acquires enormous perceived scale through that frame. |
| What made it sufficient? | The particular sound's impression is determined against the surrounding silence. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | At the footstep's perceived magnitude, completed through the setting. |
| What remains achieved? | Contextually enormous apparent sound in the stated kinds of place. |
| What would invalidate it? | Loss of that enormous impression while retain the particular setting and sound. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** a contextually determined perceptual magnitude, not mere detectability or emitted power.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** winter silence gives the enormous impression its particular frame. Remove that bearing and this frame-constituted perception is no longer the same account.
3. **Operative continuation:** a perceptual result is expressly supplied, but no new sensory capacity, changed future conduct or independently continuing acoustic function is achieved.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** retain the distant footstep and setting but remove its enormous impression; alternatively replace the frame and any retained large impression would have a different stated constitution.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** some winter settings give a distant footstep enormous perceived scale.

**What obtains through what is:** the footstep's enormous impression obtains through the quiet acoustic frame.

A bare fact that the footstep sounds large omits what that perceptual magnitude consists in here. The frame is not merely evidence for an independently fixed intensity. The achieved result is the appearance through its context, without implying physical amplification or a before/after episode of altered hearing.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **In some places, a distant footstep's enormous perceived scale is constituted through winter silence.**

**Opening constraint:** the remote sound's perceived scale in the winter setting is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** its enormous impression is determined through the surrounding quiet.
**Constitutive organisation:** the particular sound receives its perceived magnitude against that acoustic frame.
**Location of sufficiency:** the footstep's perceived scale as the privileged determination completed through the setting.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is a distant footstep's enormous impression in some winter settings; it holds as this achievement because that perceived scale obtains through the surrounding silence; removing that contextual determination would leave any retained sound without this frame-constituted impression.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** the distant sound and winter quiet remain but no enormous impression obtains.
**Confirmation:** preserves existential scope, places, winter, silence, distance and perceived scale; adds no decibels, universal rule, amplified emission or new hearing capacity; identifies one contextual perceptual determination.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**20 — Pivoted balance; Relating × Focal × Bearing.**

A particular sound acquires its perceived magnitude through the acoustic counterpart that constitutes that magnitude. The focal appearance and its frame are one achieved determination.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Relating fits perceived scale through a frame. Differentiation is the closest cross-pattern challenge, but mere detection or sound/silence distinction omits the particular magnitude-through-context identity. Opening no enlarged hearing reach; Repair no failed criterion; Integration no organised whole; Settlement no discharged demand. |
| Topology | Focal fits the particular perceived scale completed through its context. Distributed would make a field of sounds or counterpart standings govern, whereas the record identifies a privileged perceptual term. Release has no former governance losing force. |
| Persistence | Bearing fits context-constituted appearance. Standing would retain the large impression detached from its constitutive frame. Efficacy would make an achieved acoustic operation or changed continuation govern; the record expressly identifies contextual perception instead. |

### Strongest rivals

**21 — Responsive return; Relating × Focal × Efficacy.** An effect on perception can in other records be an operative consequence. Here the frozen achievement is an apparent magnitude that obtains through a frame, not a before/after alteration of hearing, an achieved sensory capacity or a subsequent course of conduct. This is a positive identity distinction, not a claim that perception can never be consequential efficacy.

**19 — Direct bearing; Relating × Focal × Standing.** Would retain the attribution as an established fact. The frozen holding statement instead makes the impression's particular scale obtain through the quiet context, not as an independently fixed magnitude.

**02 — Binary bearing; Differentiation × Focal × Bearing.** Would establish the sound's distinction against silence. The record specifies its enormous perceived scale, not just distinguishability. The frame's constitutive relation to magnitude therefore governs.

**23 — Reciprocal shift; Relating × Distributed × Bearing.** Requires completion across counterpart standings without a privileged focal term. The frozen record locates completion at this sound's perceived scale, with context determining it.

“Pivoted balance” names contextual counterpart-dependence here. No equal opposing forces, auditory equilibrium or emitted-energy increase is inferred.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 20. **Confidence:** moderate. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen account; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-027-D1:** Test the perceptual-magnitude identity against an accomplished effect on perception in 21. Do not exclude Efficacy solely because no later action is supplied; ask what the frozen result itself is.
- **A-027-D2:** Test magnitude-through-frame against distinguishability in 02, and focal determination against a distributed context in 23. Do not infer topology merely from a singular footstep.

No earlier record or revision. This completes the announced sequential batch through A-027. Next unworked sentence: A-028.

# A-028 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-028-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1 workbook, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. Exact wording verified against the live source. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. The register ends at A-027. The owner authorised another handful, announced as A-028–A-032 in sequence.

## Gate One — source analysis

> A new road shortens journeys while changing which towns people pass through.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

“A new road” identifies a newly introduced source of change. “Shortens journeys” establishes one consequence. “While changing which towns people pass through” retains that consequence and specifies another aspect of the travel changed by the same road. The completed point is not travel improvement alone: shortening comes together with altered passage through places.

1. The road shortens journeys. Omits the changed towns.
2. The road destroys the towns it bypasses. Adds bypass, destruction and a social outcome.
3. The new road changes travel jointly through shorter journeys and different towns passed through. Preserves both stated consequences.
4. The road is built in order to redirect economic activity. Invents purpose and an economic mechanism.

Candidate 3 survives. “Shortens” is not silently converted into a measured distance or travel-time claim. No universal benefit, particular town or actual traffic volume is asserted.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove the towns: shortening remains but the altered geographical passage disappears. Remove shortening: rerouting remains without the other stated consequence. |
| Alteration | Replace shortening with lengthening: the direction of one consequence changes. Keep the same towns: the second consequence is lost. |
| Narrowing | Reduce the claim to one named journey or town: adds specificity and weakens the general account. |
| Reversal | Make journeys build the road rather than the road change travel: reverses the presented direction. |
| Dependency | Both travel changes are attributed to the same road; neither is stated to cause the other. |
| Sufficiency-location | The road's influence is the common initiating source. Completion requires its two specified consequences, but no mutually coupled network or sequence of stages is asserted. |
| Minimal invalidation | Retain journey shortening but keep the towns passed through unchanged: the complete joint consequence fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Determination |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | How the new road changes travel beyond a single measure of journey length. |
| What changed? | Shorter journeys and changed towns of passage are established together. |
| What made it sufficient? | Both consequences belong to the same initiating road without either being reduced to the other. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | At the new road's influence as the common source of the two travel changes. |
| What remains achieved? | The road's joint consequential difference to journeys and towns of passage. |
| What would invalidate it? | One consequence remains while the other is absent. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** one source's joint travel consequences, not a generic advantage or two unrelated facts.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** the road and both consequences identify the particular influence. No exclusive cause, feedback or consequence-to-consequence dependency is asserted.
3. **Operative continuation:** yes; changed journeys and passage are expressly the differences established, rather than optional applications of an otherwise complete description.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** shorter journeys occur but the towns passed through do not change; the full influence attributed is not established.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** the new road shortens journeys and changes the towns passed through.

**What obtains through what is:** those joint changes in travel occur through the new road's influence.

The achieved identity is consequential change, not merely an established association or a static description held through another term. Both consequences must remain, but the record does not add coupling between them or a broader circulation mechanism.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **A new road changes travel jointly by shortening journeys and altering which towns people pass through.**

**Opening constraint:** the new road's full significance for travel is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** shortening and altered towns of passage are established as joint consequences.
**Constitutive organisation:** one initiating road has two indispensable, distinct travel consequences.
**Location of sufficiency:** the road's influence as their common source, specified through both effects.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the new road's joint consequential difference to journeys and towns of passage; it holds as this achievement because the road changes both how short journeys are and which towns are passed through; removing either consequence would leave a narrower influence than the complete travel change established.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** journeys shorten but the towns of passage remain unchanged.
**Confirmation:** preserves new road, shortening and changed towns; adds no quantitative measure, causal link between the effects, economic outcome or universal benefit; identifies one source-centred consequential account.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**21 — Responsive return; Relating × Focal × Efficacy.**

One initiating source is determinate through its joint consequences for travel; its influence completes at that source and remains as the operative difference made. Both effects belong to the same achieved influence.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Relating fits directed influence from the road to both travel changes. Opening would isolate improved travel while omit changed passage; Differentiation would retain a distinction without the consequential source; Repair adds a failed criterion; Integration adds organised joint capacity; Settlement adds discharged demand. |
| Topology | Focal fits the recorded common source of both effects. Distributed would require a constitutive system of couplings or span, not merely two consequences. Release has no former governing authority losing force. |
| Persistence | Efficacy fits the consequences expressly identified as achievement. Standing would retain a connection without the changes made. Bearing would retain a source-dependent account but omit the altered continuation itself. |

### Strongest rivals

**24 — Relational cycle; Relating × Distributed × Efficacy.** Both effects must be retained, but the record establishes neither coupling between them nor a mutually determining system. A plurality of consequences does not by itself displace the identified focal source.

**19 — Direct bearing; Relating × Focal × Standing.** Would retain an established road–travel connection. The frozen achievement is the difference made in both respects, not connection alone.

**12 — Branching output; Opening × Focal × Efficacy.** Would foreground newly improved availability. The record includes that shorter-travel aspect but governs through the joint consequential reconfiguration, including changed towns rather than an asserted expansion of access.

“Responsive return” does not require a literal reply or feedback loop. No economic benefit or harm is imported to make a story fit the name.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 21. **Confidence:** moderate. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-028-D1:** Test Focal against Distributed while preserving both effects. Do not decide from singular grammar or, conversely, from counting consequences.
- **A-028-D2:** Preserve unspecified shortening and changed towns without adding time savings, bypass losses or a coupling between the effects.

# A-029 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-029-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-028 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> The earth beneath a playground contains histories no child needs to know.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

The earth is located beneath a playground. It contains histories, giving the present site a past depth. The relative clause specifies those histories as ones no child needs to know. Existence of this past is therefore not equated with a requirement that children know it.

1. The ground contains traces of history. Omits the children's lack of need to know.
2. Children know nothing about the past. Changes unnecessary knowledge into actual ignorance.
3. The ground beneath a playground contains histories without any child's needing to know those histories. Preserves presence, setting and non-necessity.
4. Children must be protected from disturbing archaeological facts. Adds a protective purpose, disturbing content and an injunction.

Candidate 3 survives. “No child needs to know” neither says no child does know nor prohibits learning. The sentence does not give a particular past event, claim a physical artefact, or explicitly report children playing.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove the relative clause: historical presence remains but its separation from children's need to know disappears. Remove the historical presence: non-necessity has no specified content. |
| Alteration | “No child will know” changes need into prediction. “No child should know” introduces a prohibition absent from the source. |
| Narrowing | “One child need not know” weakens the universal negative scope. Replace playground with an archaeological classroom: changes the setting rather than merely restating it. |
| Reversal | Histories that every child must know reverse the determination of necessity while retaining historical presence. |
| Dependency | The histories are present, but their presence does not establish a child's need to know them. No causal or sustaining dependence between the two is asserted. |
| Sufficiency-location | Completion requires distinguishing historical presence at this site from children's epistemic necessity; neither side alone establishes the whole point. |
| Continuation restraint | No actual ignorance, learning, protected innocence or capacity for play is reported as the achieved outcome. |
| Minimal invalidation | Keep the same histories but make knowledge of them necessary for a child: the stated non-necessity fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | Whether the histories present beneath the playground entail a need for children to know them. |
| What changed? | Their presence is retained while such a need is denied for every child. |
| What made it sufficient? | Both historical depth and non-necessity of knowing are held without confusing one with the other. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | Across historical presence and children's need to know in the playground setting. |
| What remains achieved? | A determined distinction between the existence of these histories and the necessity of knowing them. |
| What would invalidate it? | Knowing these histories becomes necessary for a child while their presence remains. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** historical presence distinguished from epistemic necessity, not an account of actual ignorance.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** both terms identify this distinction, but the histories are not constituted through children's ignorance or a continuing contrast. The result is the determination that knowing is unnecessary.
3. **Operative continuation:** no accomplished ability to play without knowledge or new permission to ignore the past is established. The lack of need is not a reported liberation from a prior rule.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** retain the histories but require their knowledge for a child; the universal lack of need is defeated.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** these histories exist without any child's needing to know them.

**What obtains through what is:** the distinction is specified by considering their presence alongside children's need to know.

The second specifies the result rather than supplying an ongoing support for it. A statement of contextual poignancy might be available, but the necessary achieved determination is non-necessity of knowing, not histories made meaningful only through ignorance. No previous knowing-demand is stated as having governed and then been removed.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **Histories are present in the ground beneath a playground without any child's needing to know them, distinguishing their existence from the necessity of knowing.**

**Opening constraint:** the relation between this historical presence and children's need to know is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** presence is affirmed and epistemic necessity denied.
**Constitutive organisation:** the two domains stand distinguished in the same playground setting.
**Location of sufficiency:** across historical presence and children's need to know.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the distinction between histories being present beneath the playground and their knowledge being unnecessary for every child; it holds as this achievement because existence and epistemic necessity stand separately determined; making that knowledge necessary for a child would defeat the universal non-necessity established.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** the histories remain but a child needs to know them.
**Confirmation:** preserves earth, playground, histories and no child's need; adds neither actual ignorance, prohibition, specific events nor successful play; identifies one distinction.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**04 — Scope standing; Differentiation × Distributed × Standing.**

Historical presence and epistemic necessity become separately determinate across the stated setting, and that distinction itself remains as the result.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Differentiation fits non-conflation of existence with need to know. Relating would make a constituted connection govern, whereas the record establishes that presence does not entail necessity. Opening no enlarged access; Repair no failed criterion; Integration no common organised whole; Settlement no prior live demand discharged. |
| Topology | Distributed fits the recorded across-domain distinction. Focal would reduce completion to a site's historical attribute or an individual child's status. Release would require a previously governing demand losing force; the record explicitly does not establish that history. |
| Persistence | Standing fits the separate determinations left established. Bearing would make contextual significance through a contrast constitute the result, contrary to the explicit holding account. Efficacy would add an achieved ability, permission or changed action. |

### Strongest rivals

**05 — Context-bound mark; Differentiation × Distributed × Bearing.** Could foreground poignancy constituted through juxtaposed past and childhood. The frozen record instead identifies the distinction between presence and necessity as the complete governing determination; neither actual ignorance nor context-dependent valuation is established as its identity.

**52 — Transformation release; Settlement × Release × Standing.** Lack of a need does not alone establish discharge of a formerly live demand. The frozen record specifically withholds any previous requirement being overcome.

**01 — Focal mark; Differentiation × Focal × Standing.** A focal attribution at the site would omit the record's across-domain distinction. Neither historical content alone nor the child's status alone completes it.

“Scope standing” names an established distinction over the specified domains, not an instruction that children avoid history.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 04. **Confidence:** moderate. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-029-D1:** Test standing distinction against context-constituted significance in 05 without importing actual childhood ignorance or sentiment.
- **A-029-D2:** Check that no-demand is not mistaken for a discharged demand. Preserve the difference between unnecessary knowledge, absent knowledge and prohibited knowledge.

No earlier record or revision. Next target: A-030.

# A-030 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-030-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-029 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> When a language disappears, particular ways of noticing may become harder to inherit.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

“When a language disappears” specifies a condition. “Particular ways of noticing” identifies what may be affected, without extending to every way of thinking. “May become harder to inherit” establishes a possible worsening of transmission, not inevitable impossibility or a reported actual loss in one case.

1. A language disappears. Omits what this can mean for transmission.
2. All thought associated with a lost language vanishes irretrievably. Adds universal scope, certainty and impossibility.
3. A language's disappearance may make the inheritance of particular ways of noticing more difficult. Preserves condition, selected content, possibility and increased difficulty.
4. Losing a language creates new abilities to notice. Reverses the direction of the consequence.

Candidate 3 survives. “Inherit” concerns cultural transmission here, not a biological genetic mechanism. No particular language, community or generation is named.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove transmission difficulty: disappearance remains without its stated significance. Remove the disappearance condition: possible difficulty loses its specified source. |
| Alteration | “Impossible” overstates “harder”; “will” overstates “may”; “all ways” overstates “particular.” |
| Narrowing | A named linguistic community would add specificity, while one specified technique of noticing would add content absent from the source. |
| Reversal | Easier inheritance reverses the stated tendency. Difficult inheritance causing language disappearance changes the direction of the conditional account. |
| Dependency | Possible transmission difficulty is attributed to disappearance; language is not asserted to be the sole means of retaining those ways. |
| Sufficiency-location | The disappearance is the particular source condition for the possible transmission consequence. Multiple interacting institutions or an entire generational chain are not specified. |
| Loss test | Loss is described, but no formerly governing constraint is shown ceasing to determine how people may proceed. Disappearance alone does not establish such a liberation. |
| Minimal invalidation | Under disappearance, the stated increase in inheritance difficulty is not even possible: the attributed potential consequence fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | What disappearance can mean for inheriting particular ways of noticing. |
| What changed? | A possible increase in transmission difficulty is established under that condition. |
| What made it sufficient? | The loss condition and possible practical consequence are linked with scope and modality intact. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | At language disappearance as the particular source condition of the potential consequence. |
| What remains achieved? | The attributed potential of disappearance to hinder that inheritance. |
| What would invalidate it? | Such hindrance is not possible under the disappearance condition. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** a scoped possible impairment of transmission, not complete loss of thought or inevitable failure.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** the consequence is specified through disappearance; alternatives for transmission are not ruled out. Both source condition and selected inheritance identify the potential influence.
3. **Operative continuation:** yes; increased difficulty in what can be passed on is explicitly the possible consequence. No actual failed inheritance is required to establish the scoped potential.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** deny the possibility of the particular increased difficulty under disappearance while retaining the condition and selected content.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** disappearance may make selected forms of noticing harder to inherit.

**What obtains through what is:** possible impairment of transmission follows through the loss of the language.

The result's identity lies in what may change for transmission, rather than a bare established condition or a static description of one term through another. The consequence can be adverse and remain genuinely consequential. No beneficial opening, new permission or performed failure is added.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **A language's disappearance may make particular ways of noticing harder to inherit.**

**Opening constraint:** the disappearance's significance for transmitting those ways is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** possible increased inheritance difficulty is established through that source condition.
**Constitutive organisation:** the selected modes of noticing are connected to the language loss through a possible impairment of transmission.
**Location of sufficiency:** disappearance as the privileged source condition of the potential consequence.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the potential of language disappearance to make particular ways of noticing harder to inherit; it holds as this achievement because a practical worsening of transmission is attributed to that condition as possible; removing the possibility of that worsening would leave disappearance without the scoped consequential significance established.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** disappearance cannot make inheritance of those ways any harder.
**Confirmation:** preserves when, disappearance, particular ways, may and harder; adds no named case, exclusive transmission route, certainty or impossibility; identifies one potential consequence.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**21 — Responsive return; Relating × Focal × Efficacy.**

A particular loss condition is established through its possible practical consequence for continuation. The potential transmission difficulty is the achieved consequential bearing, not an independently added effect.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Relating fits the directed conditional influence. Opening would require greater availability, not merely an availability-related subject or a negative opening. Repair requires a fault being remedied, not merely an impairment. Differentiation would omit consequential influence; Integration no whole; Settlement no discharged demand. |
| Topology | Focal fits the particular source condition. Distributed would need a constitutive pattern across stages or systems not identified in the record. Release requires prior governance losing authority; disappearance alone does not establish that. |
| Persistence | Efficacy fits a scoped potential difference to practical transmission. Bearing would retain a conditional dependence without the potential change in continuation that defines the frozen result. Standing would retain only an established connection. |

### Strongest rivals

**20 — Pivoted balance; Relating × Focal × Bearing.** The condition is constitutive to specifying the attribution, but the frozen identity is a possible impairment in what can be transmitted. It is that practical difference, not only a condition-relative standing, that remains achieved.

**24 — Relational cycle; Relating × Distributed × Efficacy.** Cultural transmission could involve a complex system, but the record supplies no such coordinated coupling. A plural set of ways or a potentially long time does not make completion Distributed.

**27 — Emergent coordination; Relating × Release × Efficacy.** No former governing authority is overcome. Loss of a source and release from governance are distinct structures.

“Responsive return” is interpreted through focal consequentiality, not a literal answer, beneficial response or feedback loop. An adverse potential consequence can instantiate Efficacy when that difference is expressly the recorded identity.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 21. **Confidence:** medium-high in consequential modality; moderate in topology. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-030-D1:** Test potential practical impairment against a condition-relative attribution in 20. Preserve “may” rather than requiring an actual failed transmission.
- **A-030-D2:** Do not infer a full generational system from the word inheritance. Preserve selected scope and increased difficulty rather than certainty or impossibility.

No earlier record or revision. Next target: A-031.

# A-031 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-031-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-030 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> A judge's decision can outlast the room where it was first spoken.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

“A judge's decision” identifies a determination. “Can outlast” attributes possible persistence beyond another thing. “The room where it was first spoken” supplies the physical setting of its initial utterance as the comparative term.

1. A judge makes a decision. Omits possible persistence beyond the room.
2. Every judicial ruling remains legally binding forever. Adds universality, legal force and unending duration.
3. A judge's decision can persist beyond the room of its first utterance. Preserves possibility, comparative duration and initial setting.
4. Destroying a room makes a decision valid. Invents destruction as a cause and legal validity as an outcome.

Candidate 3 survives. The sentence compares possible endurance; it does not report an actual demolition, recitation, enforcement or transmission mechanism.

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove the room: possible endurance remains without the particular comparison. Remove the decision: endurance of a room alone loses the contrast with the determination first spoken there. |
| Alteration | “Must outlast” changes possibility into necessity. “Can outlast its speaker” changes the benchmark from place to person. |
| Narrowing | Restrict to a named verdict or historic building: adds specificity absent from the claim. |
| Reversal | The room can outlast the decision establishes the reverse possible ordering. |
| Dependency | The room identifies the comparison and initial utterance setting. Its survival is not asserted as a support constituting the decision's continuing identity or force. |
| Sufficiency-location | The possible duration ordering spans decision and original room; neither term alone establishes outlasting. |
| Continuation restraint | Possible persistence does not by itself establish that the decision directs later conduct or remains enforceable. |
| Minimal invalidation | The decision cannot survive beyond the original room under any relevant possibility: the attributed possible outlasting fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | How the decision's possible persistence compares with that of its initial room. |
| What changed? | Persistence beyond that physical setting is established as possible. |
| What made it sufficient? | A decision and its initial place are given a possible temporal ordering of endurance. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | Across the two durations, with the room specified by initial utterance. |
| What remains achieved? | The possible ordering in which the decision persists after the original room does not. |
| What would invalidate it? | Rule out the decision's persisting beyond that room. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** a possible comparative endurance, not an actual claim of permanent authority.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** both terms identify the ordering; the initial room does not maintain the decision's identity or supply its validity.
3. **Operative continuation:** none is specified. The possibility concerns endurance, not an expressly attributed power to change subsequent conduct.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** make outlasting impossible while retain both the decision and its original setting.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** the decision can outlast its initial physical room.

**What obtains through what is:** its possible comparative endurance is specified through the room as benchmark.

The latter identifies the ordering. It does not establish that a continuing source-bearing is the identity of the result. What remains achieved is the possible temporal arrangement itself. No source support, legal force or former room-dependence being removed is stated.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **A judge's decision can persist beyond the room in which it was first spoken.**

**Opening constraint:** the decision's possible endurance relative to its initial physical setting is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** its persistence beyond that room is established as possible.
**Constitutive organisation:** decision and initial room receive a possible ordering of endurance.
**Location of sufficiency:** across that comparative temporal span.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the possible temporal ordering in which the decision outlasts its initial room; it holds as this achievement because the two are determinately placed in that possible endurance configuration; making persistence beyond the room impossible would defeat the attributed ordering.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** the decision cannot persist beyond its original room.
**Confirmation:** preserves judge's decision, can, outlasting and first utterance setting; adds no actual destruction, mechanism, indefinite duration or enforceability; identifies one possible temporal configuration.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**22 — Sequential link; Relating × Distributed × Standing.**

A possible temporal ordering is determined across the decision and its initial physical setting, and that possible configuration itself remains available as the result.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Relating fits the comparative ordering. Differentiation would retain a contrast without the determined temporal relation. Opening would infer enlarged possibility from descriptive modality alone; Repair no corrected fault; Integration no organised whole; Settlement no live adjudication being discharged in this record. |
| Topology | Distributed fits the comparative span of two durations. Focal would reduce the result to a lone endurance attribute, losing the record's across-term completion. Release would infer former physical dependence being overcome, which the record explicitly withholds. |
| Persistence | Standing fits the possible temporal arrangement itself. Bearing would require the achieved standing to be constituted through its benchmark, beyond the recorded ordering. Efficacy would add an achieved power to direct later continuation or a specific function. |

### Strongest rivals

**23 — Reciprocal shift; Relating × Distributed × Bearing.** Both terms identify the comparison. But the frozen holding statement takes the possible ordering as its achieved identity, not decision endurance constituted through the room. The room is the benchmark and initial setting; no continuing support is established. Bearing need not be physical, so absence of physical support alone is not the criterion: the positive result account is the temporal configuration.

**19 — Direct bearing; Relating × Focal × Standing.** Could attribute endurance at a privileged term. The frozen locus of sufficiency instead spans the decision and its initial room; a standalone endurance statement cannot complete the identified ordering.

**52 — Transformation release; Settlement × Release × Standing.** The subject is a decision, but no live adjudication or former governing demand is discharged by this particular achievement. The semantic subject does not determine the transformation.

“Sequential link” names a temporal configuration without requiring a narrated chain of actions. Standing is selected from what the completed account is, not merely because the subject itself lasts.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 22. **Confidence:** medium-high in temporal ordering; moderate in topology. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-031-D1:** Test comparative ordering against benchmark-constituted endurance in 23, preserving the positive holding account rather than relying only on absence of physical support.
- **A-031-D2:** Preserve descriptive possibility without treating “can” as an automatic operative capacity. Do not infer legal force or actual enforcement from the decision's possible endurance.

No earlier record or revision. Next target: A-032.

# A-032 — first-pass resolution and diligence

**Record ID:** FRESHER-A-032-2026-10-21-01. **Date / assessor:** 21 October 2026, Europe/Dublin; Fresher / OpenAI Codex.
**Source:** frozen Corpus A §15, Edition 1.1, blob `1b511a439a5007a3c4a61c36ff5417f415005e2c`. **Protocol:** achieved-identity confirmation, blob `69eb1e465b48bbc2e0fef0d315456a6ebabd2107`; workflow blob `f522e66fd3b068e0b07cee16c8e21ee99121ebeb`. A-031 was fully saved, indexed and published before this investigation.

## Gate One — source analysis

> The mountain does not move, but every climber discovers a different approach.

### Unfolding and candidate Centres

The mountain does not move. “But” asks that this constancy be understood alongside a contrasting contribution. Each climber discovers a different approach. The same object can remain fixed while ways of approaching it differ in the climbers' discoveries.

1. The mountain stays still. Omits the discovery of differing approaches.
2. Climbers physically change the mountain. Contradicts the supplied constancy.
3. The mountain remains fixed while each climber discovers a different way of approaching it, distinguishing object constancy from variation in approach. Preserves the whole contrast.
4. Every climber creates a physically unique route that no one else could use. Adds creation, exclusive ownership and a uniqueness criterion.

Candidate 3 survives. “Approach” can concern a way toward or of engaging the mountain. No narrow physical route geometry, purely psychological theory or mathematical proof that all routes are pairwise unique is required to preserve the governing contrast. “Discovers” is not silently replaced with “creates.”

### Verification tests

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| Deletion | Remove the mountain's stillness: varied discoveries remain without the contrast of a constant object. Remove the climbers: physical constancy remains without varied approach. |
| Alteration | Make all discoveries the same approach: variation is lost. Make the mountain move to offer each way: variation no longer coexists with the supplied fixed object. |
| Narrowing | One climber alone does not preserve the statement's across-climber variation. A specific route or psychological interpretation would add an unnecessary restriction. |
| Reversal | A changing mountain with an unchanging approach exchanges which aspect varies and establishes a different point. |
| Dependency | Approach variation is distinguished from object movement. The mountain is not constituted as unmoving through comparison, and an approach need not be brought into existence by its discovery. |
| Sufficiency-location | The distinction requires the same mountain and differing climber approaches across the cases. Neither an isolated still mountain nor one isolated discovery establishes it. |
| Availability test | No prior impasse, inaccessible mountain or newly enlarged stock of routes is asserted. Discovery of difference is not itself a claim that the mountain has acquired more routes. |
| Minimal invalidation | Retain the fixed mountain but remove variation among the approaches discovered; the complete contrast fails. |

### Six confirmation questions

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What was unresolved? | Whether constancy of the mountain coincides with constancy of ways of approaching it. |
| What changed? | The mountain's fixity and varied approaches are distinguished while kept in one account. |
| What made it sufficient? | The unchanged object is retained across the climbers' differing discoveries. |
| Where is sufficiency located? | Across the constant object and variation among the approaches. |
| What remains achieved? | The distinction between fixity of the mountain and variability of approaching it. |
| What would invalidate it? | The approaches cease to differ while the mountain remains fixed. |

### Four holding tests

1. **Identity:** an established contrast of object constancy with approach variation, including discoveries by the climbers.
2. **Constitutive dependencies:** both aspects identify this contrast, but neither constancy nor the existence of approaches is constituted through a sustaining counterpart. Discovery does not entail creation.
3. **Operative continuation:** discovery is included, but no newly enabled route, improved climbing capacity, successful ascent or changed subsequent conduct is established.
4. **Minimal invalidation:** retain the unmoving object while the approaches discovered no longer differ; one side remains without the contrasted result.

### Achieved-identity discrimination

**What has been established is:** a fixed mountain coexists with differing approaches discovered by climbers.

**What obtains through what is:** the distinction is specified through comparing constant object with variable approach.

The comparison establishes the distinction rather than sustaining either term's identity through the other. An alternative claim that each approach exists only through a climber's perspective would add constitution beyond discovery. The achieved result is the distinction left determined, not a theory of observer-created reality.

### Frozen Centre Confirmation Record

**Confirmed Operative Centre:** **The mountain remains fixed while each climber discovers a different approach, distinguishing constancy of the object from variation in approaching it.**

**Opening constraint:** the relation between object constancy and approach constancy is unspecified.
**Achieved completion:** fixity and approach variation stand distinguished within one account.
**Constitutive organisation:** the same object is retained across differing discoveries without making its movement the source of difference.
**Location of sufficiency:** across the fixed mountain and the differing climber approaches.
**Mode of holding:** **What remains achieved is the distinction between the mountain's constancy and variation in the approaches discovered; it holds as this achievement because the same fixed object and different approaches stand determinately contrasted; removing variation while retaining the fixed object would defeat this contrasted configuration.**
**Decisive counterfactual:** all approaches discovered are the same while the mountain remains fixed.
**Confirmation:** preserves immobility, every climber, discovery and difference; adds no created route, successful ascent, inaccessible starting point or mandatory reading of approach; identifies one contrast.

**OPERATIVE CENTRE CONFIRMED — SOURCE SENTENCE CLOSED.**

## Gate Two — frozen record and canonical reference only

**Canonical authority:** THE_54_CONTROL.md, blob `113cdf0c50a2eb8645189f0505fa8a9584028322`.

### Unified conjunction

**04 — Scope standing; Differentiation × Distributed × Standing.**

The object's constancy becomes distinct from variation in approaching it across the cases, and that contrasted configuration is the result retained.

### Structural alternatives

| Dimension | Assessment from the frozen record |
|---|---|
| Transformation | Differentiation fits keeping object fixity distinct from approach variation. Relating would make correspondence itself govern, whereas the record makes this distinction the completed point. Opening would add enlarged access or newly executable paths; Repair no failed criterion; Integration no roles in a whole; Settlement no discharged demand. |
| Topology | Distributed fits the contrast across the same object and differing approaches. Focal would gather completion at one discovery or object attribute, each insufficient to this record. Release has no former governance losing force. |
| Persistence | Standing fits the contrasted configuration itself. Bearing would require perspective-constituted identity or continuing comparative significance beyond the frozen distinction. Efficacy would infer a changed climbing capacity, successful route or future action. |

### Strongest rivals

**05 — Context-bound mark; Differentiation × Distributed × Bearing.** The contrast is indispensable, but the frozen account identifies an established distinction, not mountain or approach identities constituted through counterpart perspectives. The result formulation positively captures the complete configuration.

**22 — Sequential link; Relating × Distributed × Standing.** Would establish a relation or arrangement. The record's governing achievement is specifically the non-conflation of object constancy with approach constancy, rather than a sequence or correspondence of independently specified approaches.

**13 — Alternate passage; Opening × Distributed × Standing.** The name resembles different approaches, but the frozen record supplies neither a prior restriction nor a larger repertoire becoming available. Name resemblance cannot supply an opening.

“Scope standing” describes a distinction established across the cases; it is not selected from the physical mountain's size or persistence.

### Diligence and status

**Status:** candidate 04. **Confidence:** moderate. **Uniqueness:** selected under this frozen record; independent reproducibility is not established.
- **A-032-D1:** Test the established contrast against contextual constitution in 05 without replacing discovery with observer-created approaches.
- **A-032-D2:** The route/perspective breadth of approach is preserved because both support the recorded structure. A materially different source reading would require a separately preserved Centre revision, not post-freeze source appeal.

No earlier record or revision. This completes the announced handful A-028–A-032. Next unworked sentence: A-033.

## Edition 1 addendum — A-033 (21 October 2026)

**Research status:** 33/200 first-pass investigations; A-007 revision 02 remains a separate revision. **Next: A-034.** This append-only addendum does not retroactively alter the original Edition 1 case texts or canonical definitions. [Complete A-033 frozen record and Gate Two diligence](laboratory/resolutions/A-033.md); [live register](laboratory/RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md); [public receipt](sentence-receipts.html#A-033).

**A-033 exact source:** “Trade winds helped connect distant shores long before airplanes crossed the oceans.”

**Frozen Operative Centre:** “Trade winds' assistance in connecting distant shores is established as an accomplishment already occurring substantially before ocean-crossing aviation.” The full frozen confirmation record, six answers, counterfactuals and holding tests are preserved verbatim in the linked case file. The connecting contribution is what receives temporal determination; the comparison establishes its substantial historical precedence. Full candidate Centre formulations proved alternative emphases on one achievement, not three rival achievements. The One-Way Threshold was declared before taxonomy.

**Gate Two:** **Candidate 22 — Sequential link; Relating × Distributed × Standing**; **nearest rival 23 — Reciprocal shift (Bearing)**; **secondary topology rival 19 — Direct bearing (Focal)**. Moderate candidate confidence; **uniqueness not established**. This was a collaborative, tutored, non-blind investigation, **not independent replication**.

**Methodological lessons from the preserved investigation:**
- Historical operative assistance needs no present-day continuation or additional downstream effect to count as achieved.
- Do not infer governing precedence solely because a comparison completes the sentence, or governing contribution solely because the contribution is independently intelligible.
- Before declaring two Centres rivals, ask what each full account actually entails that the other omits. A joint Centre must explain the organisation, not merely list indispensable terms.
- A constitutive temporal comparison need not be causal; requiring two terms for a comparison does not itself decide persistence.
- **Bearing** does not require an ongoing condition or a further sustaining process; constitutive contrast or frame can suffice.
- **Focal** is not excluded because other contributions are necessary. The positive Distributed argument must establish a constitutive temporal span as the locus of completion; A-033 retains this as an open topology question.
- The distinction between a historical determination that stands and a result held through constitutive bearing is still open to independent challenge. Do not overstate uniqueness.

**Diligence queue:** A-033-D1 Standing/Bearing; A-033-D2 Distributed/Focal; A-033-D3 independent blind replication; A-033-D4 separately preserved Gate One revision if a future source-based repair is genuinely needed. The live research file and register control all later status updates.

## A-034 — one source, four possibilities; corrected Centre and Cell 12 (21 October 2026)

**Exact sentence:** “An open door can mean welcome, escape, opportunity, or a forgotten obligation.”

**Confirmed, corrected Gate One Centre:** **One open door can give rise to four distinct possibilities of meaning: welcome, escape, opportunity, and forgotten obligation.**

**Discovery:** The initial investigation added “without its actual significance being determined,” inventing a selection problem. Owner challenge established that the sentence's positive achievement is **one thing capable of four significances**. The door need not literally intend or communicate; the figurative human intelligibility of the possible meanings does not require a reported interpreter. The earlier Centre was prematurely frozen; the source-based correction is explicitly recorded, and only the corrected Centre governs final Gate Two.

**Gate Two as one simultaneous conjunction:** **Opening × Focal × Efficacy**. Opening: multiple possible meanings become available through one open condition. Focal: the one open door is the privileged locus; the four possibilities exhibit its capacity, rather than serving as four independent loci. Efficacy: what holds is the accomplished capacity of the one condition to mean several things, without any actual enactment being required.

**Canonical cell:** **12 — Branching output** (coordinate-generated form *Focal generative opening*; companion utterance “More ways open.”).

**Nearest rivals, each at full strength:** 15 (Opening × Distributed × Efficacy) preserves the opening capacity but moves completion from the one source to the range; 13 (Opening × Distributed × Standing) makes the range stand rather than holding as focal capacity; 05 (Differentiation × Distributed × Bearing) captures different meanings through their common attribution but does not govern the branching achievement; 04 (Differentiation × Distributed × Standing) captures plurality of distinctions but makes distinction, rather than opening from one source, the achievement.

**Teaching principle:** The Operative Centre is where the three axes are born together. Express each rival as a complete sentence close to the original, then ask whether it preserves the exact achieved identity. Do not infer a selection problem merely from multiple possibilities, or infer Distributed merely by counting outputs. Do not let the labels determine the Centre.

**Complete source investigation:** [A-034](laboratory/resolutions/A-034.md). **Corpus progress:** 34/200; next A-035.
